<!-- page 1 -->
NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Tuesday, February 11, 2020
ONE OR MORE SUPERVISORS MAY ATTEND VIA VIDEO AND/OR TELEPHONICALLY 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors will hold a meeting open to the public on Tuesday, February 11, 2020, in the Supervisors’
Chamber in the Navajo County Governmental Complex, Holbrook, Arizona. The Board may vote to meet in a closed executive
session to discuss certain matters as indicated on the following agenda. The Board may meet in executive session pursuant to
A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (3) for legal advice on any item.
Persons with disabilities who need accommodation to attend or participate in the meeting may contact the Clerk of the Board’s office
at (928) 524-4053; at least 48 hours prior to the meeting so an accommodation can be arranged.
Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairman’s discretion. A copy of the agenda background material
provided to the Board, with the exception of items to be considered in executive session, is available for public inspection at Navajo
County website, www.navajocountyaz.gov 
9:00 a.m. Call To Order:  Invocation and Pledge of Allegiance
           
1. CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Individuals may address the Board on any relevant issue for up to 5
minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Board members may not respond to any comments
but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a matter be placed on a
future agenda.
 
 
2. CONSENT AGENDA: These items are considered to be routine or administrative in nature and
will be approved in a single motion. A Board member may request for any item to be removed
from the consent agenda and considered on the regular agenda:
 
 
a. Minutes: January 28, 2020  
 
b. Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000, January 2020  
 
c. Constable Reports: Snowflake Precinct #3, Kayenta Precinct #4, Show Low Precinct #5 and
Pinetop-Lakeside Precinct #6, January 2020
 
 
d. Contracts signed by County Manager/Assistant County Manager pursuant to Board of
Supervisors Resolution: Professional Service Contract for Social Media regarding Census
 
 
e. Fiscal Year 19/20 Fund Transfers for November and December 2019  
 
f. Appointment of Lewis G. Carlassara, Thomas Allen Davis, Sherry M. Davis, Michael Paul
Riendeau, Billy Joel Smith, John Benjamin Dorsett and Kenneth Leon Garvin Jr. as Republican
Precinct Committeemen for their respective precincts
 
 
g. Land Purchase Agreement for the procurement of right-of-way from landowners Ava and Larry
Baldwin for a portion of Obed Road that crosses private property associated with APN
#107-20-018 and 107-21-009A

<!-- page 2 -->
3. BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
 
a. Ceremonial Crowning of the Navajo County Rodeo Queens: Codi Ross, Rodeo Queen, Tova
Conner, Teen Queen, and Macy Brimhall, Little Buckaroo. 
 
Presented By: Board of Supervisors 
 
4. NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICE DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session:  
 
a. REGULAR AGENDA:  
 
1. Presentation update on the current activites related to the 2019 Coronavirus  
Presented By: Jeff Lee
 
5. TREASURER:  
 
a. Consideration and possible approval of the proposed settlement in TX-2019-001726,
CenturyLink v. ADOR & Navajo County et. al.
 
Presented By: Jason Moore
 
6. ELECTIONS:  
 
a. Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution Number 11-2020 Polling Places/Vote
Centers for the Presidential Preference Election and Poll Workers on March 17, 2020.
 
Presented By: Rayleen Richards
 
7. WHITE MOUNTAIN LAKES COUNTY RECREATION IMPROVEMENT DISTRICT:
Board of Directors Session:
 
 
a. REGULAR AGENDA  
 
1. Presentation of Quarterly Report  
Presented By: Patricia Stevens
 
8. COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from County Manager, County Attorney and Board
members.
 
 
 
9. ADJOURN  
 
Dated: February 6, 2020 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
Posted: 4:36 p.m. By: MB                        
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. f.        
Meeting Date: 02/11/2020  
Title: Elections - REP PC Appoint
Submitted By: Rayleen Richards, Elections Director
Department: Elections
Motion before the Board:
Appointment of Lewis G. Carlassara, Thomas Allen Davis, Sherry M. Davis, Michael Paul
Riendeau, Billy Joel Smith, John Benjamin Dorsett and Kenneth Leon Garvin Jr. as Republican
Precinct Committeemen for their respective precincts
Background:
Appointment valid through Oct. 1, 2020. 16-822. Precinct committeemen; eligibility; vacancy;
duties; term B.  If the number of persons who file nominating petitions for an election to fill
precinct committeeman positions is less than or equal to the number of precinct committeeman
positions, the county board of supervisors may cancel the election for those positions not sooner
than seventy-five days before the election and appoint the person who filed the nominating
petition to fill the position. If no person has filed a nominating petition to fill a position, the
position is deemed vacant and shall be filled as otherwise provided by law. A precinct
committeeman who is appointed pursuant to this subsection after filing a nominating petition shall
be deemed an elected precinct committeeman. F. The term of office of a precinct committeeman is
two years and begins on October 1 after the primary election at which the precinct committeeman
was a candidate and continues until October 1 after the following primary election at which a
precinct committeeman is elected.
Attachments
REP PC Appointment 2-11-2020

Title: Obed Road Right-of-Way: Land Purchase Agreement
Submitted For: Bill Bess, County Engineer/Assistant Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Donna Hurstrom, Deputy Clerk of the Board
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Land Purchase Agreement for the procurement of right-of-way from landowners Ava and Larry
Baldwin for a portion of Obed Road that crosses private property associated with APN
#107-20-018 and 107-21-009A
Background:
This land purchase agreement is for the procurement of right-of-way from landowners Ava and
Larry Baldwin for a portion of Obed Road that crosses private property associated with APN #
107-20-018 and 107-21-009A for the amount of $1,600 for 0.92 combined acres.
An independent party, KB Valuation Services, was used to appraise the property for $1,600.
Navajo County will responsible for the relocation of the right-of-way fence.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY19
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: 1,600.99
Fiscal Impact:
The right-of-way agreement for the procurement of private property located on Obed Road is
expected to cost $1,600.99. This will come from HURF as right-of-way procurement.

Board of Supervisors Regular 6. a.        
Meeting Date: 02/11/2020  
Title: March 17 2020 PPE Poll Workers and Polling Place
Submitted By: Rayleen Richards, Elections Director
Department: Elections
Presented By: Rayleen Richards
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution Number 11-2020 Polling Places/Vote Centers
for the Presidential Preference Election and Poll Workers on March 17, 2020.
Background:
Pursuant to A.R.S. § 16-531
  
When an election is ordered, and not less than twenty days prior to a general or primary
election, the board of supervisors shall appoint for each election precinct one inspector, one
marshal, two judges and as many clerks of election as deemed necessary.
A.
 
Pursuant to A.R.S. § 16-411
  
Not less than twenty days before a general or primary election, and at least ten days
before a special election, the board shall designate one polling place within each
precinct where the election shall be held, except that:
B.
 
                   1. On a specific finding of the board, included in the order or resolution designating
polling places pursuant to this subsection, that no suitable polling place is available within a
precinct, a polling place for such precinct may be designated within an adjacent precinct.
Recommendation: 
Staff Recommends Approval.

RESOLUTION NO ______ - 20 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS, 
DESIGNATING POLLING PLACES/VOTE CENTERS, PURSUANT TO ARS § 16-411(B). 
 
 WHEREAS, ARS § 16-411(B) provides as follows with respect to designating polling places: 
 
         “Not less than twenty days before a general or primary election, and at least ten days before a special election, the 
board shall designate one polling place within each precinct where the election shall be held, except that: 1.On a specific 
finding of the board, included in the order or resolution designating polling places pursuant to this subsection, that no 
suitable polling place is available within a precinct, a polling place for such precinct may be designated within an adjacent 
precinct. 
         and 
 
WHEREAS, on a specific resolution of the board, the board may authorize the use of voting centers in place of or in addition 
to specifically designated polling place. A voting center shall allow any voter in the county to receive the appropriate ballot 
for that voter on Election Day and lawfully cast the ballot. The voting center may be established in coordination and 
consultation with the county recorder, at other county offices or at other location in the county deemed appropriate. 
       
  
 NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED by the Board of Supervisors as follows:  
 
 That polling locations/vote centers for all Navajo County voting precincts attached hereto and incorporated herein as 
“Exhibit A”, are hereby approved by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors. 
 
           PASSED, APPROVED AND ADOPTED at Holbrook, Arizona on Feb. 11, 2020 
 
  
 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
 
                
 By ________________________________________ 
 Jason Whiting 
 Chairman of the Board

Totals
New Business
We currently have 12 wet docks. All but two are reserved for the 2020 season and there is a wait list 
of 9 more. Had considered adding more wet dock space to accommodate some on the wait list but 
there are concerns regarding the number of boats on the lake now as well as the lake area that 
would be taken up by more slips. More information needs to be gathered before deciding if more 
wet slips should be added.
Silver Creek Irrigation District (SCID) Agreement: I was invited to a meeting between our member of 
the BOD (Jason Whiting) in October to discuss the White Mountain Lake DIstrict Agreement 
(WMLCRID ) with SCID. I was unable to attend but understand that the SCID was asked to submit 
copies of their recent electric bills and repairs to the five pumps they use when the lake level reaches 
the agreement level of 24 feet.To date, I believe we are still waiting for the receipts from SCID.
There is a need to re-visit the lake's liability policy to make certain there is coverage for anyone 
injured while swimming, boating, etc.
Proposed development:  Members are still expressing concerns over the 
influx of traffic, law enforcement, tresspassing, etc.  At the lake - we are 
already understaffed, porta jons only currently support the traffic we have; 
we will need to purchase, or rent more. Concerns continue regarding the 
parking problem and the potential for a child to be injured.  Another 
concern  is the fact that lake use rules are all based on ownership - not 
property renters.430
0 Still determining the best way to move the docks in/out on the ramps as 
the water level changes.0
0 Parking is an ongoing issue; had planned to extend parking south, past the 
shed. With the sale and then purchase of the property adjacent to the 
south side of our parking lot by a private entity this project has been put on 
hold. First to determine the survey lines and now weather.
